Map cluster to region via channel assignment\n   222→    cluster_regions = chan_beryl[clusters['channels']]\n   223→    return cluster_regions\n   224→\n   225→\n   226→def bin_spikes_vectorized(spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n   227→    \"\"\"Bin spikes into trial-aligned time windows. Optimized implementation.\"\"\"\n   228→    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n   229→    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n   230→\n   231→    # Pre-sort spikes are already time-sorted after merge\n   232→    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n   233→        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n   234→        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n   235→\n   236→        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n   237→            continue\n   238→\n   239→        # Use searchsorted for fast interval selection\n   240→        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_start, side='left')\n   241→        idx_end = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_end, side='left')\n   242→\n   243→        if idx_beg >= idx_end:\n   244→            continue\n   245→\n   246→        t_sel = spike_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   247→        c_sel = spike_clusters[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   248→\n   249→        # Compute bin indices\n   250→        bin_idx = np.minimum(((t_sel - t_start) / binsize).astype(np.int32), n_bins - 1)\n   251→\n   252→        # Use flat indexing for efficient accumulation\n   253→        valid = (c_sel >= 0) & (c_sel < n_clusters)\n   254→        flat_idx = c_sel[valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n   255→        np.add.at(binned[trial_idx].ravel(), flat_idx, 1)\n   256→\n   257→    return binned\n   258→\n   259→\n   260→def interpolate_wheel(timestamps, position, fs=WHEEL_FS, corner_freq=WHEEL_CORNER_FREQ, order=WHEEL_FILTER_ORDER):\n   261→    \"\"\"Interpolate wheel position and compute velocity (matching ibllib).\"\"\"\n   262→    t = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\n   263→    if len(t) > 0 and t[-1] > timestamps[-1]:\n   264→        t = t[:-1]\n   265→\n   266→    pos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t)\n   267→\n   268→    # Butterworth low-pass filter for velocity\n   269→    sos = signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_freq / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n   270→    vel = np.insert(np.diff(signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n   271→\n   272→    return t, np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)  # wheel speed = |velocity|\n   273→\n   274→\n   275→def load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left'):\n   276→    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy from camera data.\"\"\"\n   277→    if side == 'left':\n   278→        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n   279→        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n   280→    else:\n   281→        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n   282→        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n   283→\n   284→    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n   285→        return None, None\n   286→\n   287→    me = np.load(me_file).flatten()\n   288→    times = np.load(times_file).flatten()\n   289→\n   290→    # Handle length mismatch (common in IBL data)\n   291→    min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n   292→    return times[:min_len], me[:min_len].astype(np.float32)\n   293→\n   294→\n   295→def interpolate_behavior_to_bins(beh_times, beh_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n   296→    \"\"\"Interpolate behavior signal to trial-aligned bins (matching reference code).\"\"\"\n   297→    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n   298→    result = np.full((n_trials, n_bins), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n   299→    good_mask = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n   300→\n   301→    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n   302→        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n   303→        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n   304→\n   305→        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n   306→            continue\n   307→\n   308→        # Find behavior data in this interval\n   309→        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_start, side='right')\n   310→        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n   311→\n   312→        t_sel = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   313→        v_sel = beh_values[idx_beg:idx_end]\n   314→\n   315→        if len(v_sel) == 0:\n   316→            continue\n   317→\n   318→        # Check data availability (matching reference code)\n   319→        if np.abs(t_start - t_sel[0]) > binsize:\n   320→            continue\n   321→        if np.abs(t_end - t_sel[-1]) > binsize:\n   322→            continue\n   323→\n   324→        # Interpolate to uniform bins (matching reference code)\n   325→        x_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n   326→        try:\n   327→            y_interp = interp1d(t_sel, v_sel, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n   328→            result[trial_idx] = y_interp\n   329→            good_mask[trial_idx] = True\n   330→        except Exception:\n   331→            continue\n   332→\n   333→    return result, good_mask\n   334→\n   335→\n   336→def discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=N_DISCRETE_BINS):\n   337→    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins equal-frequency bins using quantiles.\n   338→    Computed across all valid (non-NaN) values.\"\"\"\n   339→    valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n   340→    if len(valid) == 0:\n   341→        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=int), np.array([])\n   342→\n   343→    # Compute quantile boundaries\n   344→    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n   345→    boundaries = np.percentile(valid, quantiles)\n   346→\n   347→    # Digitize - np.digitize returns values 0..n_bins, clip to 0..n_bins-1\n   348→    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries[1:-1])  # 0 to n_bins-1\n   349→    result = np.clip(result, 0, n_bins - 1)\n   350→\n   351→    return result.astype(int), boundaries\n   352→\n   353→\n   354→def compute_trial_in_block(prob_left):\n   355→    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n   356→    Block boundaries are where probabilityLeft changes.\"\"\"\n   357→    trial_in_block = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n   358→    count = 1\n   359→    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n   360→        if i > 0 and prob_left[i] != prob_left[i-1]:\n   361→            count = 1\n   362→        trial_in_block[i] = count\n   363→        count += 1\n   364→    return trial_in_block\n   365→\n   366→\n   367→def process_session(session_info, br, show_processing=False):\n   368→    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\"\"\"\n   369→    eid = session_info['eid']\n   370→    subject = session_info['subject']\n   371→    lab = session_info['lab']\n   372→    date = session_info['date']\n   373→\n   374→    t0 = time.time()\n   375→\n   376→    # Check revision directories (format: #YYYY-MM-DD#)\n    candidates = []\n    if base_path.exists():\n        for item in base_path.iterdir():\n            if item.is_dir() and item.name.startswith('#') and item.name.endswith('#'):\n                for f in item.iterdir():\n                    if filename_pattern in f.name:\n                        candidates.append(f)\n        # Also check base path directly\n        for f in base_path.iterdir():\n            if not f.is_dir() and filename_pattern in f.name:\n                candidates.append(f)\n\n    if not candidates:\n        return None\n    # Sort by revision date (newest first), files without revision come last\n    def sort_key(p):\n        parts = p.parts\n        for part in parts:\n            if part.startswith('#') and part.endswith('#'):\n                return part\n        return '#0000-00-00#'\n    candidates.sort(key=sort_key, reverse=True)\n    return candidates[0]\n\n\ndef load_trials(alf_path):\n    \"\"\"Load trials table from session.\"\"\"\n    trials_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_trials.table.pqt')\n    if trials_file is None:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No trials table found in {alf_path}\")\n    trials = pd.read_parquet(trials_file)\n    return trials\n\n\ndef create_trial_mask(trials):\n    \"\"\"Create boolean mask for trial inclusion following reference code.\"\"\"\n    mask = pd.Series(True, index=trials.index)\n\n    # Exclude NaN in key events\n    for event in NAN_EXCLUDE:\n        if event in trials.columns:\n            mask &= ~trials[event].isna()\n\n    # Reaction time filter\n    rt = trials['firstMovement_times'] - trials['stimOn_times']\n    mask &= (rt >= MIN_RT) & (rt <= MAX_RT)\n\n    # Exclude no-choice trials\n    mask &= (trials['choice'] != 0)\n\n    # Max trial length filter\n    if 'goCue_times' in trials.columns:\n        trial_len = trials['feedback_times'] - trials['goCue_times']\n        mask &= (trial_len <= MAX_TRIAL_LEN) | trial_len.isna()\n\n    return mask\n\n\ndef load_spike_sorting(alf_path, probe_name):\n    \"\"\"Load spike sorting data for a probe.\"\"\"\n    probe_path = alf_path / probe_name / 'pykilosort'\n\n    # Find latest revision\n    spike_times_file = find_latest_revision(probe_path, 'spikes.times.npy')\n    if spike_times_file is None:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"No spike times found in {probe_path}\")\n\n    rev_dir = spike_times_file.parent\n\n    spikes = {\n        'times': np.load(rev_dir / 'spikes.times.npy').flatten(),\n        'clusters': np.load(rev_dir / 'spikes.clusters.npy').flatten(),\n    }\n\n    # Load cluster info\n    clusters_channels = np.load(rev_dir / 'clusters.channels.npy').flatten()\n    clusters_depths = np.load(rev_dir / 'clusters.depths.npy').flatten()\n\n    # Load cluster metrics\n    metrics_file = rev_dir / 'clusters.metrics.pqt'\n    if metrics_file.exists():\n        metrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_file)\n    else:\n        metrics = None\n\n    # Load channel brain region IDs\n    chan_brain_ids = np.load(rev_dir / 'channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy').flatten()\n\n    clusters = {\n        'channels': clusters_channels,\n        'depths': clusters_depths,\n        'metrics': metrics,\n        'chan_brain_ids': chan_brain_ids,\n    }\n\n    return spikes, clusters\n\n\ndef merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list):\n    \"\"\"Merge spike data from multiple probes.\"\"\"\n    if len(spikes_list) == 1:\n        return spikes_list[0], clusters_list[0]\n\n    merged_spikes_times = []\n    merged_spikes_clusters = []\n    merged_channels = []\n    merged_depths = []\n    merged_chan_brain_ids_list = []\n    merged_metrics_list = []\n\n    cluster_offset = 0\n    chan_offset = 0\n\n    for spikes, clusters in zip(spikes_list, clusters_list):\n        n_clusters = len(clusters['channels'])\n\n        merged_spikes_times.append(spikes['times'])\n        merged_spikes_clusters.append(spikes['clusters'] + cluster_offset)\n        merged_channels.append(clusters['channels'] + chan_offset)\n        merged_depths.append(clusters['depths'])\n        merged_chan_brain_ids_list.append(clusters['chan_brain_ids'])\n\n        if clusters['metrics'] is not None:\n            m = clusters['metrics'].copy()\n            if 'cluster_id' in m.columns:\n                m['cluster_id'] += cluster_offset\n            merged_metrics_list.append(m)\n\n        cluster_offset += n_clusters\n        chan_offset += len(clusters['chan_brain_ids'])\n\n    # Concatenate\n    all_times = np.concatenate(merged_spikes_times)\n    all_clusters = np.concatenate(merged_spikes_clusters)\n\n    # Sort by time\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(all_times, kind='stable')\n\n    merged_spikes = {\n        'times': all_times[sort_idx],\n        'clusters': all_clusters[sort_idx],\n    }\n\n    all_chan_brain_ids = np.concatenate(merged_chan_brain_ids_list)\n\n    merged_clusters = {\n        'channels': np.concatenate(merged_channels),\n        'depths': np.concatenate(merged_depths),\n        'chan_brain_ids': all_chan_brain_ids,\n        'metrics': pd.concat(merged_metrics_list, ignore_index=True) if merged_metrics_list else None,\n    }\n\n    return merged_spikes, merged_clusters\n\n\ndef get_brain_regions(clusters, br):\n    \"\"\"Get Beryl brain region acronyms for each cluster.\"\"\"\n    chan_ids = clusters['chan_brain_ids']\n    chan_acronyms = br.id2acronym(chan_ids)\n    chan_beryl = br.acronym2acronym(chan_acronyms, mapping='Beryl')\n\n    # Map cluster to region via channel assignment\n    cluster_regions = chan_beryl[clusters['channels']]\n    return cluster_regions\n\n\ndef bin_spikes_vectorized(spike_times, spike_clusters, n_clusters, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n    \"\"\"Bin spikes into trial-aligned time windows. Optimized implementation.\"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n    binned = np.zeros((n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    # Pre-sort spikes are already time-sorted after merge\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            continue\n\n        # Use searchsorted for fast interval selection\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_start, side='left')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(spike_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        if idx_beg >= idx_end:\n            continue\n\n        t_sel = spike_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        c_sel = spike_clusters[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        # Compute bin indices\n        bin_idx = np.minimum(((t_sel - t_start) / binsize).astype(np.int32), n_bins - 1)\n\n        # Use flat indexing for efficient accumulation\n        valid = (c_sel >= 0) & (c_sel < n_clusters)\n        flat_idx = c_sel[valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n        np.add.at(binned[trial_idx].ravel(), flat_idx, 1)\n\n    return binned\n\n\ndef interpolate_wheel(timestamps, position, fs=WHEEL_FS, corner_freq=WHEEL_CORNER_FREQ, order=WHEEL_FILTER_ORDER):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate wheel position and compute velocity (matching ibllib).\"\"\"\n    t = np.arange(timestamps[0], timestamps[-1], 1.0 / fs)\n    if len(t) > 0 and t[-1] > timestamps[-1]:\n        t = t[:-1]\n\n    pos_interp = interp1d(timestamps, position, kind='linear')(t)\n\n    # Butterworth low-pass filter for velocity\n    sos = signal.butter(N=order, Wn=corner_freq / fs * 2, btype='lowpass', output='sos')\n    vel = np.insert(np.diff(signal.sosfiltfilt(sos, pos_interp)), 0, 0) * fs\n\n    return t, np.abs(vel).astype(np.float32)  # wheel speed = |velocity|\n\n\ndef load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left'):\n    \"\"\"Load whisker motion energy from camera data.\"\"\"\n    if side == 'left':\n        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy')\n    else:\n        me_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, 'rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy')\n        times_file = find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy')\n\n    if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n        return None, None\n\n    me = np.load(me_file).flatten()\n    times = np.load(times_file).flatten()\n\n    # Handle length mismatch (common in IBL data)\n    min_len = min(len(me), len(times))\n    return times[:min_len], me[:min_len].astype(np.float32)\n\n\ndef interpolate_behavior_to_bins(beh_times, beh_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, binsize, n_bins):\n    \"\"\"Interpolate behavior signal to trial-aligned bins (matching reference code).\"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(interval_starts)\n    result = np.full((n_trials, n_bins), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    good_mask = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n\n    for trial_idx in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = interval_starts[trial_idx]\n        t_end = interval_ends[trial_idx]\n\n        if np.isnan(t_start) or np.isnan(t_end):\n            continue\n\n        # Find behavior data in this interval\n        idx_beg = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_start, side='right')\n        idx_end = np.searchsorted(beh_times, t_end, side='left')\n\n        t_sel = beh_times[idx_beg:idx_end]\n        v_sel = beh_values[idx_beg:idx_end]\n\n        if len(v_sel) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Check data availability (matching reference code)\n        if np.abs(t_start - t_sel[0]) > binsize:\n            continue\n        if np.abs(t_end - t_sel[-1]) > binsize:\n            continue\n\n        # Interpolate to uniform bins (matching reference code)\n        x_interp = np.linspace(t_start + binsize, t_end, n_bins)\n        try:\n            y_interp = interp1d(t_sel, v_sel, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')(x_interp)\n            result[trial_idx] = y_interp\n            good_mask[trial_idx] = True\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n\n    return result, good_mask\n\n\ndef discretize_to_bins(values, n_bins=N_DISCRETE_BINS):\n    \"\"\"Discretize continuous values into n_bins equal-frequency bins using quantiles.\n    Computed across all valid (non-NaN) values.\"\"\"\n    valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n    if len(valid) == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(values, dtype=int), np.array([])\n\n    # Compute quantile boundaries\n    quantiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n    boundaries = np.percentile(valid, quantiles)\n\n    # Digitize - np.digitize returns values 0..n_bins, clip to 0..n_bins-1\n    result = np.digitize(values, boundaries[1:-1])  # 0 to n_bins-1\n    result = np.clip(result, 0, n_bins - 1)\n\n    return result.astype(int), boundaries\n\n\ndef compute_trial_in_block(prob_left):\n    \"\"\"Compute trial number within each block.\n    Block boundaries are where probabilityLeft changes.\"\"\"\n    trial_in_block = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n    count = 1\n    for i in range(len(prob_left)):\n        if i > 0 and prob_left[i] != prob_left[i-1]:\n            count = 1\n        trial_in_block[i] = count\n        count += 1\n    return trial_in_block\n\n\ndef process_session(session_info, br, show_processing=False):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session and return data dict.\"\"\"\n    eid = session_info['eid']\n    subject = session_info['subject']\n    lab = session_info['lab']\n    date = session_info['date']\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    # Find session path\n    alf_path = find_session_path(lab, subject, date)\n    if alf_path is None:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid} ({subject}/{date}): path not found, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    # Load trials\n    try:\n        trials = load_trials(alf_path)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading trials: {e}\")\n        return None\n\n    # Create trial mask\n    mask = create_trial_mask(trials)\n\n    # Find probes\n    probe_dirs = sorted([d.name for d in alf_path.iterdir() if d.is_dir() and d.name.startswith('probe')])\n    if not probe_dirs:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: no probes found, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    # Load and merge spike sorting from all probes\n    spikes_list = []\n    clusters_list = []\n    for probe_name in probe_dirs:\n        try:\n            spk, clu = load_spike_sorting(alf_path, probe_name)\n            spikes_list.append(spk)\n            clusters_list.append(clu)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  Session {eid}, {probe_name}: error loading spikes: {e}\")\n            continue\n\n    if not spikes_list:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: no valid probes, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    spikes, clusters = merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)\n    n_clusters = len(clusters['channels'])\n\n    # Get brain regions\n    cluster_regions = get_brain_regions(clusters, br)\n\n    # Apply trial mask\n    valid_trials = trials[mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: fewer than 2 valid trials, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    # Compute intervals aligned to stimOn_times\n    align_times = valid_trials[ALIGN_TIME].values\n    interval_starts = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + TIME_WINDOW[1]\n\n    # Bin spikes\n    binned_spikes = bin_spikes_vectorized(\n        spikes['times'], spikes['clusters'], n_clusters,\n        interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n    )\n    # binned_spikes shape: (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n\n    # Load wheel data\n    wheel_speed_binned = None\n    try:\n        wheel_pos_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.position.npy')).flatten()\n        wheel_ts_raw = np.load(find_latest_revision(alf_path, '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy')).flatten()\n        wheel_times, wheel_speed = interpolate_wheel(wheel_ts_raw, wheel_pos_raw)\n        wheel_speed_binned, wheel_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n            wheel_times, wheel_speed, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n        )\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading wheel: {e}\")\n\n    # Load whisker motion energy\n    me_binned = None\n    try:\n        me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='left')\n        if me_times is None:\n            me_times, me_values = load_motion_energy(alf_path, side='right')\n        if me_times is not None:\n            me_binned, me_good = interpolate_behavior_to_bins(\n                me_times, me_values, interval_starts, interval_ends, BINSIZE, N_BINS\n            )\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: error loading motion energy: {e}\")\n\n    # Build combined mask (trials where all data is available)\n    combined_mask = np.ones(len(valid_trials), dtype=bool)\n    if wheel_speed_binned is not None:\n        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(wheel_speed_binned), axis=1)\n    else:\n        # If no wheel data, mark all as bad\n        combined_mask[:] = False\n\n    if me_binned is not None:\n        combined_mask &= ~np.any(np.isnan(me_binned), axis=1)\n    else:\n        combined_mask[:] = False\n\n    # Apply combined mask\n    n_valid = combined_mask.sum()\n    if n_valid < 2:\n        print(f\"  Session {eid}: fewer than 2 trials with all data, skipping\")\n        return None\n\n    final_spikes = binned_spikes[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins)\n    final_wheel = wheel_speed_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n    final_me = me_binned[combined_mask]  # (n_trials, n_bins)\n    final_trials = valid_trials[combined_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    # Build neural data: list of (n_neurons, n_timepoints) per trial\n    # Use uint8 to save memory (spike counts per 20ms bin are small integers, max ~12)\n    neural_trials = []\n    for t in range(len(final_spikes)):\n        neural_trials.append(final_spikes[t].astype(np.uint8))  # (n_clusters, n_bins)\n\n    # Build inputs\n    # Input 0: time since stimulus onset (continuous, time-varying)\n    time_since_stim = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS).astype(np.float32)\n\n    # Input 1: trial number in block (continuous, per-trial)\n    prob_left_all = trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n    trial_in_block_all = compute_trial_in_block(prob_left_all)\n    # Apply masks to get trial_in_block for valid+combined trials\n    trial_in_block_valid = trial_in_block_all[mask.values]\n    trial_in_block_final = trial_in_block_valid[combined_mask]\n\n    input_trials = []\n    for t in range(len(final_trials)):\n        inp = np.array([\n            time_since_stim,  # time-varying\n            np.full(N_BINS, trial_in_block_final[t], dtype=np.float32),  # per-trial, broadcast to time\n        ], dtype=np.float32)  # (2, n_bins)\n        input_trials.append(inp)\n\n    # Build outputs\n    # Output 0: choice (binary, per-trial) - left=0, right=1\n    choice = final_trials['choice'].values.copy()\n    # IBL: -1=left, 1=right -> convert to 0=left, 1=right\n    choice_encoded = ((choice + 1) // 2).astype(int)  # -1->0, 1->1\n\n    # Output 1: prior probability of left (per-trial, categorical)\n    prob_left = final_trials['probabilityLeft'].values\n    prior_encoded = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=int)\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.2] = 0\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.5] = 1\n    prior_encoded[prob_left == 0.8] = 2\n\n    # Output 2: wheel speed discretized into 3 bins (time-varying)\n    # Discretize using quantiles across ALL timepoints in this session\n    all_wheel_flat = final_wheel.flatten()\n    wheel_disc, wheel_boundaries = discretize_to_bins(all_wheel_flat, N_DISCRETE_BINS)\n    wheel_disc_2d = wheel_disc.reshape(final_wheel.shape).astype(int)\n\n    # Output 3: whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins (time-varying)\n    all_me_flat = final_me.flatten()\n    me_disc, me_boundaries = discretize_to_bins(all_me_flat, N_DISCRETE_BINS)\n    me_disc_2d = me_disc.reshape(final_me.shape).astype(int)\n\n    output_trials = []\n    for t in range(len(final_trials)):\n        out = np.array([\n            np.full(N_BINS, choice_encoded[t], dtype=int),  # per-trial, broadcast\n            np.full(N_BINS, prior_encoded[t], dtype=int),  # per-trial, broadcast\n            wheel_disc_2d[t],  # time-varying\n            me_disc_2d[t],  # time-varying\n        ], dtype=int)  # (4, n_bins)\n        output_trials.append(out)\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n    print(f\"  Session {eid} ({subject}/{date}): {n_valid} trials, {n_clusters} neurons, {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n    # Processing visualization\n    if show_processing:\n        try:\n            plot_processing(eid, subject, date, final_spikes, final_wheel, final_me,\n                          wheel_disc_2d, me_disc_2d, choice_encoded, prior_encoded,\n                          time_since_stim, cluster_regions)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  Warning: could not create processing plots: {e}\")\n\n    return {\n        'neural': neural_trials,\n        'input': input_trials,\n        'output': output_trials,\n        'subject': subject,\n        'cluster_regions': cluster_regions,\n        'n_clusters': n_clusters,\n        'n_trials': n_valid,\n        'eid': eid,\n    }\n\n\ndef plot_processing(eid, subject, date, spikes, wheel, me, wheel_disc, me_disc,\n                   choice, prior, time_axis, cluster_regions):\n    \"\"\"Plot processing steps for visual verification.\"\"\"\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(4, 3, figsize=(18, 16))\n    fig.suptitle(f'Processing: {subject}/{date} ({eid[:8]}...)', fontsize=14)\n\n    # Trial 0 and 1\n    for col, trial_idx in enumerate([0, min(1, len(spikes)-1)]):\n        # Neural activity (first 50 neurons)\n        n_show = min(50, spikes.shape[1])\n        axes[0, col].imshow(spikes[trial_idx, :n_show, :], aspect='auto', cmap='hot',\n                           extent=[time_axis[0], time_axis[-1], n_show, 0])\n        axes[0, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Neural ({n_show}/{spikes.shape[1]} neurons)')\n        axes[0, col].set_xlabel('Time from stimOn (s)')\n\n        # Wheel speed (raw vs discretized)\n        axes[1, col].plot(time_axis, wheel[trial_idx], 'b-', alpha=0.5, label='Raw wheel speed')\n        axes[1, col].plot(time_axis, wheel_disc[trial_idx], 'r-', label='Discretized')\n        axes[1, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Wheel speed')\n        axes[1, col].legend(fontsize=8)\n\n        # Whisker ME (raw vs discretized)\n        axes[2, col].plot(time_axis, me[trial_idx], 'b-', alpha=0.5, label='Raw ME')\n        axes[2, col].plot(time_axis, me_disc[trial_idx], 'r-', label='Discretized')\n        axes[2, col].set_title(f'Trial {trial_idx}: Whisker ME')\n        axes[2, col].legend(fontsize=8)\n\n    # Summary statistics\n    # Choice distribution\n    axes[0, 2].bar(['Left (0)', 'Right (1)'],\n                   [np.sum(choice == 0), np.sum(choice == 1)])\n    axes[0, 2].set_title('Choice distribution')\n\n    # Prior distribution\n    axes[1, 2].bar(['0.2 (0)', '0.5 (1)', '0.8 (2)'],\n                   [np.sum(prior == 0), np.sum(prior == 1), np.sum(prior == 2)])\n    axes[1, 2].set_title('Prior distribution')\n\n    # Wheel speed histogram\n    axes[2, 2].hist(wheel.flatten(), bins=50, alpha=0.7)\n    axes[2, 2].set_title('Wheel speed distribution')\n\n    # Brain regions\n    unique_regions, counts = np.unique(cluster_regions, return_counts=True)\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(-counts)[:15]  # Top 15\n    axes[3, 0].barh(range(len(sort_idx)), counts[sort_idx])\n    axes[3, 0].set_yticks(range(len(sort_idx)))\n    axes[3, 0].set_yticklabels(unique_regions[sort_idx], fontsize=7)\n    axes[3, 0].set_title('Top brain regions')\n\n    # ME distribution\n    axes[3, 1].hist(me.flatten(), bins=50, alpha=0.7)\n    axes[3, 1].set_title('Whisker ME distribution')\n\n    # Mean neural activity across trials\n    axes[3, 2].plot(time_axis, spikes.mean(axis=(0, 1)), 'k-')\n    axes[3, 2].set_title('Mean firing rate')\n    axes[3, 2].set_xlabel('Time from stimOn (s)')\n\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    safe_eid = eid[:8]\n    fig.savefig(f'/app/processing_{safe_eid}.png', dpi=150)\n    plt.close(fig)\n    print(f\"  Saved processing plot: processing_{safe_eid}.png\")\n\n\ndef get_session_list(bwm_df):\n    \"\"\"Get unique sessions from BWM release CSV.\"\"\"\n    # Group by eid to get one row per session\n    sessions = bwm_df.groupby('eid').first().reset_index()\n    return sessions\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    parser.add_argument('output', type=str, help='Output pickle file path')\n    parser.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True, help='Process all sessions (default)')\n    parser.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot processing visualizations')\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    if args.sample:\n        args.full = False\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"IBL Brain-Wide Map Data Conversion\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    t_start = time.time()\n\n    # Load BWM release info\n    bwm_df = pd.read_csv(BWM_CSV, index_col=0)\n    sessions = get_session_list(bwm_df)\n    print(f\"Total sessions in BWM release: {len(sessions)}\")\n\n    if args.sample:\n        # Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **papers**:\n    27→  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n    28→  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n    29→  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n    30→- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    31→- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n    32→- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   175→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   176→3. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference papers and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **papers**:\n  - \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\" describes how the data were collected and is in the file `datapaper.pdf`.\n  - \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\" describes how the code were used and is in the file `methodpaper.pdf`.\n  - A white paper about the data architecture is in `dataarchitecture.pdf\n- Some parts of the papers that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the methods paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the data paper are in the directory `data`. Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step. When you find mistakes, document and fix them immediately. Be critical of results **after every step of processing**. \n\nYou will be assessed on whether the decisions you make on how to load, filter, process, align, reformat, and save the data are reasonable and match the reference paper and code and these instructions. During testing, the reference version of `train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n\n---\n\n## Conversion Workflow\n\n**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Each step has explicit completion criteria. Do not proceed to the next step until the current step is complete. Use the exact step names below when documenting progress in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n**STATUS TRACKING**: At the start of each step, update its status to `IN PROGRESS` in CONVERSION_NOTES.md. When complete, update to `COMPLETE`. This helps you and the user track progress.\n\n---\n\n### Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n\n**Goal**: Set up the working environment and create the notes file.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. **FIRST**: Create `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` with the template structure shown at the end of this document\n2. Verify you can run `python3` and import key packages (numpy, torch)\n3. List the contents of this directory to understand what files are available\n4. **REMINDER**: Do NOT look at any files outside this directory\n\n**Done when**: \n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists with the proper template structure\n- You have confirmed Python and key packages work\n- You have listed directory contents in CONVERSION_NOTES.md\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 1, verify that `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` exists by running `ls -la CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. If it doesn't exist, STOP and create it now.\n\n---\n\n### Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at code in the `code` directory. Do NOT look outside this project directory.\n\n**Goal**: Understand how the original authors loaded and processed their data.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Look for documentation or README files in the `code` directory\n- Read the provided reference code bases to see how the reference paper loaded and manipulated the data\n- Understand which functions are being called to read, curate, process, and manipulate the data\n- For neural imaging data, does delta F over F need to be computed? \n- For electrophysiology neural data, do cells need to be filtered based on quality? \n- Note to file important functions, their inputs, and outputs\n\n**Done when**: You have documented key functions and their purposes in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 1\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 2, update Step 1 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n\n**⚠️ CONSTRAINT REMINDER**: Only look at data in the `data` directory. Important functions from exploring the reference code\n  - Dataset structure from exploring the dataset\n  - Analysis described in the reference texts\n- Note and investigate any discrepancies\n- In particular, look for discrepancies in:\n  - Experimental and behavioral variables\n  - Data processing\n  - Data curation\n- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n\n**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n\n**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Map source data elements to target format components:\n  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n  - Temporal alignment of trials\n  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n  - Brain regions recorded from\n- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n\n**Done when**:\n- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n\n**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Script Development\n\n**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n- Include options:\n  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n- Write efficient code:\n  - Vectorize loops\n  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n  - There are no temporal misalignments\n  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n- Write clear, well-documented functions\n- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n\n**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\n---\n\n### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n  a. Verify no errors reported.\n  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n\n**Done when**: \n- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- Processing plots show no anomalies\n- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n\n**Investigate any issues**:\n- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n\n**Done when**: \n- Decoder training completes without errors\n- Loss decreases over epochs\n- Accuracies are above chance\n- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n\n---\n\n### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n\n**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n\n**Done when**:\n- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n\n---\n\n### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n\n**Check 1: Output log verification**\nRead through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n- Fix any mismatches. \n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n\n**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference papers. \n- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n- Fix any discovered issues. \n- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n\n**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\nWrite a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n\n**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n\n---\n\n### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n\n**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n\n**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n\n**Actions**:\n1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the papers. \n\n**Done when**: \n1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n\n**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n\n**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n\n**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to papers**\n- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference papers\n- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the papers's accuracy for each variable\n- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n\n**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n\n**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and papers. \n\n**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n\n**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n\n**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n\n---\n\n### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n\n**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n\n**Actions**:\n- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n  - Key decisions and rationale\n  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n  - Validation results and tables\n  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n  - Dataset description\n  - How to load and use the converted data\n  - Output format specification\n  - Key statistics\n- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n\n**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n\n---\n\n## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n\n**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. Default is False\n    39→    qc: float\n    40→        Quality threshold to be used to select good clusters. Default is None.\n    41→        If use all available clusters, set qc to None. If use good clusters, set qc to 1.\n    42→    kwargs:\n    43→        Keyword arguments passed to SpikeSortingLoader upon initiation. Specifically, if one instance offline,\n    44→        you need to pass 'eid' and 'pname' here as they cannot be inferred from pid in offline mode.\n    45→\n    46→    Returns\n    47→    -------\n    48→    selected_spikes: dict\n    49→        Spike trains associated with clusters. Dictionary with keys ['depths', 'times', 'clusters', 'amps']\n    50→    selected_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\n    51→        Information of clusters for this pid \n    52→    sampling_freq: float\n    53→        Sampling frequency of spiking data (action potential)\n    54→    \"\"\"\n    55→    eid = kwargs.pop('eid', '')\n    56→    pname = kwargs.pop('pname', '')\n    57→    spike_loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\n    58→    sampling_freq = spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band=\"ap\", stream=True).fs\n    59→    \n    60→    spikes, clusters, channels = spike_loader.load_spike_sorting()\n    61→    clusters_labeled = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\n    62→        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=compute_metrics).to_df()\n    63→    if qc is None:\n    64→        return spikes, clusters_labeled, sampling_freq\n    65→    else:\n    66→        iok = clusters_labeled['label'] >= qc\n    67→        selected_clusters = clusters_labeled[iok]\n    68→        spike_idx, ib = ismember(spikes['clusters'], selected_clusters.index)\n    69→        selected_clusters.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)\n    70→        selected_spikes = {k: v[spike_idx] for k, v in spikes.items()}\n    71→        selected_spikes['clusters'] = selected_clusters.index[ib].astype(np.int32)\n    72→        return selected_spikes, selected_clusters, sampling_freq\n    73→\n    74→\n    75→def merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list):\n    76→    \"\"\"\n    77→    Merge spikes and clusters information from several probes as if they were recorded from the same probe.\n    78→    This can be used to account for the fact that data from the probes recorded in the same session are not\n    79→    statistically independent as they have the same underlying behaviour.\n    80→\n    81→    NOTE: The clusters dataframe will be re-indexed to avoid duplicated indices. 126→    return onsets\n   127→\n   128→\n   129→def movements(t, pos, freq=1000, pos_thresh=8, t_thresh=.2, min_gap=.1, pos_thresh_onset=1.5,\n   130→              min_dur=.05, make_plots=False):\n   131→    \"\"\"\n   132→    Detect wheel movements.\n   133→\n   134→    Parameters\n   135→    ----------\n   136→    t : array_like\n   137→        An array of evenly sampled wheel timestamps in absolute seconds\n   138→    pos : array_like\n   139→        An array of evenly sampled wheel positions\n   140→    freq : int\n   141→        The sampling rate of the wheel data\n   142→    pos_thresh : float\n   143→        The minimum required movement during the t_thresh window to be considered part of a\n   144→        movement\n   145→    t_thresh : float\n   146→        The time window over which to check whether the pos_thresh has been crossed\n   147→    min_gap : float\n   148→        The minimum time between one movement's offset and another movement's onset in order to be\n   149→        considered separate.  Movements with a gap smaller than this are 'stictched together'\n   150→    pos_thresh_onset : float\n   151→        A lower threshold for finding precise onset times.  The first position of each movement\n   152→        transition that is this much bigger than the starting position is considered the onset\n   153→    min_dur : float\n   154→        The minimum duration of a valid movement.  Detected movements shorter than this are ignored\n   155→    make_plots : boolean\n   156→        Plot trace of position and velocity, showing detected onsets and offsets\n   157→\n   158→    Returns\n   159→    -------\n   160→    onsets : np.ndarray\n   161→        Timestamps of detected movement onsets\n   162→    offsets : np.ndarray\n   163→        Timestamps of detected movement offsets\n   164→    peak_amps : np.ndarray\n   165→        The absolute maximum amplitude of each detected movement, relative to onset position\n   166→    peak_vel_times : np.ndarray\n   167→        Timestamps of peak velocity for each detected movement\n   168→    \"\"\"\n   169→    # Wheel position must be evenly sampled.\n   170→    dt = np.diff(t)\n   171→    assert np.all(np.abs(dt - dt.mean()) < 1e-10), 'Values not evenly sampled'\n   172→\n   173→    # Convert the time threshold into number of samples given the sampling frequency\n   174→    t_thresh_samps = int(np.round(t_thresh * freq))\n   175→    max_disp = np.empty(t.size, dtype=float)  # initialize array of total wheel displacement\n   176→\n   177→    # Calculate a Hankel matrix of size t_thresh_samps in batches.  This is effectively a\n   178→    # sliding window within which we look for changes in position greater than pos_thresh\n   179→    BATCH_SIZE = 10000  # do this in batches in order to keep memory usage reasonable\n   180→    c = 0  # index of 'window' position\n   181→    while True:\n   182→        i2proc = np.arange(BATCH_SIZE) + c\n   183→        i2proc = i2proc[i2proc < t.size]\n   184→        w2e = hankel(pos[i2proc], np.full(t_thresh_samps, np.nan))\n   185→        # Below is the total change in position for each window\n   186→        max_disp[i2proc] = np.nanmax(w2e, axis=1) - for each window is the change in position greater than our threshold?\n   192→    moving = np.insert(moving, 0, False)  # First sample should always be not moving to ensure we have an onset\n   193→    moving[-1] = False  # Likewise, ensure we always end on an offset\n   194→\n   195→    onset_samps = np.where(~moving[:-1] & moving[1:])[0]\n   196→    offset_samps = np.where(moving[:-1] & ~moving[1:])[0]\n   197→    too_short = np.where((onset_samps[1:] - offset_samps[:-1]) / freq < min_gap)[0]\n   198→    for p in too_short:\n   199→        moving[offset_samps[p]:onset_samps[p + 1] + 1] = True\n   200→\n   201→    onset_samps = np.where(~moving[:-1] & moving[1:])[0]\n   202→    onsets_disp_arr = np.empty((onset_samps.size, t_thresh_samps))\n   203→    c = 0\n   204→    cwt = 0\n   205→    while onset_samps.size != 0:\n   206→        i2proc = np.arange(BATCH_SIZE) + c\n   207→        icomm = np.intersect1d(i2proc[:-t_thresh_samps - 1], onset_samps, assume_unique=True)\n   208→        itpltz = np.intersect1d(i2proc[:-t_thresh_samps - 1], onset_samps,\n   209→                                return_indices=True, assume_unique=True)[1]\n   210→        i2proc = i2proc[i2proc < t.size]\n   211→        if icomm.size > 0:\n   212→            w2e = hankel(pos[i2proc], np.full(t_thresh_samps, np.nan))\n   213→            w2e = np.abs((w2e.T - w2e[:, 0]).T)\n   214→            onsets_disp_arr[cwt + np.arange(icomm.size), :] = w2e[itpltz, :]\n   215→            cwt += icomm.size\n   216→        c += BATCH_SIZE - t_thresh_samps\n   217→        if i2proc[-1] >= onset_samps[-1]:\n   218→            break\n   219→\n   220→    has_onset = onsets_disp_arr > pos_thresh_onset\n   221→    A = np.argmin(np.fliplr(has_onset).T, axis=0)\n   222→    onset_lags = onset_samps[np.insert(~gap_too_small, 0, True)]\n   240→        offsets = offsets[np.append(~gap_too_small, True)]  # always keep last offset\n   241→        offset_samps = offset_samps[np.append(~gap_too_small, True)]\n   242→\n   243→    # Calculate the peak amplitudes -\n   244→    # the maximum absolute value of the difference from the onset position\n   245→    peaks = (pos[m + np.abs(pos[m:n] - pos[m]).argmax()] - pos[m]\n   246→             for m, n in zip(onset_samps, offset_samps))\n   247→    peak_amps = np.fromiter(peaks, dtype=float, count=onsets.size)\n   248→    N = 10  # Number of points in the Gaussian\n   249→    STDEV = 1.8  # Equivalent to a width factor (alpha value) of 2.5\n   250→    gauss = scipy.signal.windows.gaussian(N, STDEV)  # A 10-point Gaussian window of a given s.d.\n   251→    vel = scipy.signal.convolve(np.diff(np.insert(pos, 0, 0)), gauss, mode='same')\n   252→    # For each movement period, find the timestamp where the absolute velocity was greatest\n   253→    peaks = (t[m + np.abs(vel[m:n]).argmax()] for m, n in zip(onset_samps, offset_samps))\n   254→    peak_vel_times = np.fromiter(peaks, dtype=float, count=onsets.size)\n   255→\n   256→    if make_plots:\n   257→        fig, axes = plt.subplots(nrows=2, sharex='all')\n   258→        indices = ax.add_collection(LineCollection(t_split[0::2], colors='k'))  # Not moving\n   269→\n   270→        axes[1].autoscale()  # rescale after adding line collections\n   271→        axes[0].autoscale()\n
